PHASE A IS DONE AND THE EXPLICIT POSITIVE TABLE IS COMPLETE AT SIXTEEN OF SIXTEEN. The sponsored control that TAKES CHANGE was accepted at the pinned node, 1636 bytes handed over and the node's own copy of the mined transaction read back EQUAL to them, weight 2872, mined at height 6 with the relay boundary crossed before the mine. The change role is read OUT OF THE NODE'S COPY rather than off the request and located by the deployment's own sponsor-change program rather than by counting -- position 2, value 1000, reserve asset -- because a position is what a shape degraded to the without-change form would still have and the program is what it would not; a with-change run that reached a node carrying no change output is a HARD STOP in the ceremony rather than an acceptance reported under a row it says nothing about. Both owner signatures were taken out of that copy and verified against messages recomputed here, per input rather than per transaction, and the sponsor adapter's exact two witness items were found in the mined bytes. THE PREDICTED OBSTACLE WAS NOT THE OBSTACLE, and running is what decided it rather than more reading. One reading said the demonstration's sponsor-change program symbol is a fixture pattern no program hashes to, so a control taking change would die at its own change-role check the way the first sponsored controls died at the fee-role check. The other said the two symbols only LOOK alike: the fee role's program is target-structural, so construction wrote the empty program while the symbol was arbitrary and the two disagreed, whereas the change role's program is a deployment's own choice and construction writes the change output FROM the symbol, refusing outright if a sponsor capability offers any other destination. The second survived. The control was accepted AT THE FIRST ATTEMPT with the symbol untouched: nothing threaded, nothing repointed, the committed taptree unmoved. What was actually missing was an OFFER that carries change, and the whole fix is a sponsor funding step that funds ABOVE the fee -- the construction places a change output only where the offer states a change amount, and an offer can only state one where the coin holds more than the fee. THE CEREMONY IS LIFTED and the lift has its own control. It lived inside an integration test, where it could answer exactly one row because a test cannot be called from another test; it is now a lane with ONE axis, whether the sponsor asks for change, holding the issuance, the receipts, the destinations, the owners and the fee equal so the difference between the two submissions is attributable to the change role. The without-change member REPRODUCED at 1480 bytes and weight 2482 after the move, which is the lift's own byte-identity proof: a lift that changed what the ceremony builds would have produced a different identity. The two identities are asserted DISTINCT, so a with-change run that silently degraded fails rather than passing quietly, and the change role's measured width is stated as the 156 bytes the node charged for it rather than bounded. The duplicated ceremony leaves the test that no longer builds one, which keeps its three deployment-symbol claims that need no node. TWO STALE GROUNDS ARE CORRECTED. The wave-7 closeout's reading that the confidential fixture vocabulary has no fee role is stale -- the role exists and REQUIRES the empty program -- and the correction is recorded at the role's own site rather than in the closeout, because a closeout is history and history is not edited. And the E5 row of the confidential-funding guide rested private-sponsor-values on SponsorEnvelopeSignerAbsent, which is cleared; its live ground is the explicit/confidential gap, plus the arithmetic that a confidential sponsor value requires change at all. THE SPONSOR-AUTHORIZATION NEGATIVE IS ANSWERED, and answered behind an acceptance rather than merely recorded. One run offered a sponsored control whose sponsor input carries NO authorization and then the unmutated control, to one node on one chain. The mutant is not a second ceremony: it is the RECORDING pass's own completion, one finalization with every owner really signing and the sponsor capability answering with an empty stack, so it differs from its control in the sponsor witness and in nothing else -- measured at the node, which weighed it at 1375 bytes against the control's 1480, short by exactly the seventy-two-byte signature and thirty-three-byte key the adapter returned. The target refused it at ScriptPathRejection with mandatory-script-verify-flag-failed (Script failed an OP_EQUALVERIFY operation) and then accepted the control. THE VERDICT READS LIKE AN EARLIER WAVE'S AND IS NOT IT, which is worth saying because the string is identical: that one was the covenant's own fee-role comparison before the fee digest was threaded. What separates them is the control accepted in the SAME run -- the fee-role check passes for this deployment, so what failed is a comparison the sponsor witness reaches, and the admitted sponsor program being the target's version-zero key hash, an EMPTY item where the key goes makes the hash of nothing meet the committed digest before any signature is judged. The mutants-first order is kept for the reason the previous wave learned it: a witness mutation leaves the identity alone, so a mined control makes its own mutant read txn-already-known at a layer before script evaluation. THE THIRD OBSERVED REFUSAL is counted in its own census bucket beside the two witness-content rows. THE recognize_sponsors ASYMMETRY IS ASSESSED AND THE ANSWER IS INTENDED, decided by running rather than by more reading. The spike flagged it as possibly unintended: a receipt's value form is gated against the representation plan and a sponsor's is not, with no prose either way. Closing it is what proved it is not a gap -- the mirror clause was written, and it immediately refused the disclosure-minimality pair registry's own SPONSOR pair, whose PRIVATE member deliberately carries an EXPLICIT sponsor value. The reason is section 1.9's: a representation plan is about the PROTOCOL region, and the sponsor region is kept outside every protocol claim -- reserve asset, outside both balance equations, and section 10.7's isolation fragment introspects no value field in it at all, a property checked on the emitted instructions. A clause there would not enforce section 6.3 but extend it over a region it was written to exclude. The clause is REVERTED, the reasoning is recorded at the site, and a test now holds the shape that proves it: private receipts carrying commitments, sponsored by a reserve coin whose value is explicit. THE THIRD READING THIS WAVE OVERTURNED BY RUNNING, and the first of the three was this wave's own. PHASE B TAKES A TYPED STOP AND PHASE C STOPS ON IT. No ceremony funds a sponsor coin whose value is blinded, and the path is FILED as a typed value carrying its site inventory rather than as prose. What the arithmetic does NOT need is recorded too, because it is the cheap half and a later wave should not re-derive it: the transaction-wide solve subtracts freely chosen Primary blinders from the input blinder sum and holds the fee out at a zero blinder, and the blinder term does not depend on which asset an output carries, so a blinded sponsor change is simply a Primary and the existing single balancing election absorbs the residue with no second election and no new role; the destination intents already carry a PER-OUTPUT asset, so a reserve-asset blinded change needs no widening there either. What it does need was read at each site rather than predicted: the private lane refuses a sponsored request at its entry and passes a sponsor count of zero to shape selection, its openings vocabulary indexes inputs against the receipts alone, its destination intents are built from the receipt destinations, and -- the structural one, which is not a widening of anything -- it has NO sponsor signing stage at all, its control assembly building one witness per receipt record with no sponsor slot and no capability call. Outside the lane the funding-region classifier needs a region member as well as both of its clauses, and the executor funds and signs its sponsor coin explicitly throughout with no blinding key. THE CLASSIFIER'S OWN ORDERING IS NOW A RUN rather than a reading of the branch order: a blinded sponsor coin trips the explicitness clause FIRST, which MASKS the proof clause, so a repair that fixed only the refusal it saw would meet the second at once. Since Phase B stops, the section 15.2 private-sponsor-values row does NOT move and no confidential sponsor claim is made anywhere; what it is no longer blocked on is its arithmetic precondition, the with-change shape, which now runs. |
| `T5-049` | DONE | Sponsor-values wave: the CONFIDENTIAL sponsor funding form, taking the typed stop the sponsor-change wave filed and driving the sponsored confidential WITH-CHANGE shape to an acceptance of its own shape. The sponsor input's ASSET stays EXPLICIT because the covenant reads it and the reserve-asset guard must keep something to read, and the fee stays explicit because consensus defines a fee by its explicitness; only VALUES blind. The section 15.2 private-sponsor-values row moves ONLY on an observed acceptance of the sponsored confidential with-change shape at the pinned node, and any layer that cannot pass takes a TYPED STOP with its path filed rather than being narrated around. THE CLASSIFIER LAYER IS DONE AND THE MASKING IS GONE. The funding-region classifier decided a member's region LAST, after one explicit-only rule that every non-protocol member met whatever region it actually belonged to, which is why a blinded sponsor coin tripped the explicitness clause first and masked the proof clause it also tripped. The region is now decided on the PROGRAM ALONE -- the one property none of the clauses judge, which is what keeps the decision from depending on its own outcome -- and each clause became a rule of the region that owns it. The sponsor's reserve coin has that region and four rules of its own, each separately refusable and each with a mutant: a committed value, the range proof a committed value requires, an explicit asset the isolation fragment can introspect, and the empty surjection proof an explicit asset entails. The program is passed in rather than inferred, because a sponsor's coin and the funding party's change are alike in BOTH properties the classifier could otherwise use and telling them apart by value form would decide the region from the very field the region governs. The two explicit-only refusals are now each reachable on a mutant that breaks one rule and no other, so neither stands in front of the other. Conformance lane green at 228.8 seconds. THE ABSORBER CLAIM IS RUN RATHER THAN ARGUED AGAIN, and the materializer's own reading of the spike is CORRECTED. The spike said the destination intents already carry a per-output asset so a reserve-asset change needs no widening there: half right and half not, because the TYPE carries one and both the caller and the validator wrote the protocol asset over it. The materializer folded every destination into ONE protocol asset and refused a set that was not single-asset, so a sponsored confidential transfer -- protocol asset in the receipts, reserve asset in the sponsor's change and its fee -- was INEXPRESSIBLE rather than merely unbuilt, and the refusal it died at is named. The sponsor's change is now a DECLARED DESTINATION POSITION and not a second region, carrying its own asset, and two answers moved out of inline matches onto the role itself so the semantic balance and the asset agreement read one answer rather than each deciding it. The protocol balance of a sponsored transaction is the equation it would be if the sponsor were not there, which is section 1.9 rather than a convenience. The claim itself is executed: a sponsored candidate materializes and the balancing output's blinder is the solve over BOTH freely chosen blinders, with the test asserting first that the two candidate solves are DIFFERENT values so a run that had quietly left the sponsor change out of the subtraction fails rather than passing on a coincidence. No existing path moves, because all three existing roles answer the protocol-member question the same way they always did. Transaction lane green at 13.6 seconds, 265 of 265. THE WHOLE SPONSORED CONFIDENTIAL SHAPE NOW MATERIALIZES IN THIS WORKSPACE, end to end: a blinded-value sponsor coin consumed beside the receipts, a blinded sponsor change returned, an explicit fee paid, blinded receipt destinations, and every ASSET field explicit throughout. What a node thinks of it is a separate question and no claim is made about it here; what is settled is that the construction EXISTS, which it did not, the materializer having refused the shape outright as an asset disagreement among its own destinations. The consumed side is declared rather than inferred: a sponsor's coin could be told from a receipt by comparing its asset against the protocol asset, and that would make the protocol balance depend on a comparison the balance is itself trying to decide, so the caller says which coin it offered through a constructor that means it. The sponsor coin's AMOUNT is out of the protocol subtotal and its BLINDER is in the transaction-wide sum, and that asymmetry is the arithmetic rather than an oversight -- the blinder equation does not know which asset a term came from. A design the spike left open is settled by reading rather than by adding machinery: NO scalar summation is needed in the transaction crate, which carries no bignum and may not acquire one, because the sponsor's coin is an output of its OWN registered funding fixture and its blinder is the registry's derivation like every other opening. Transaction lane green at 9.4 seconds, 266 of 266. THE WAVE TAKES A TYPED STOP AT THE EXECUTOR LAYER AND THE SECTION 15.2 ROW DOES NOT MOVE. The stop is a BUDGET stop at a named layer rather than a discovered impossibility, and it is said that way rather than dressed as a blocker: nothing in what remains was found to be impossible, and no confidential sponsor claim is made anywhere. No ceremony yet funds a sponsor coin whose value is blinded, so A_BLINDED_SPONSOR_VALUE_IS_FUNDED_ANYWHERE stays FALSE and the row it carries stays unmoved -- the row moves only on an observed acceptance of its own shape, and no shape was offered to a node. The filed path is REFILED rather than restated, and it is narrower: what stands is the construction, and what remains is named at each site. The executor funds and signs its sponsor coin explicitly throughout, and fund_confidential is the working template that caches NOTHING, which is the one gap it does not close, a coin that will be SPENT needing its commitment retained where the receipt path never spends one; a blinded sponsor coin also needs a balancing output in its own funding transaction, its single input today being an explicit change coin at a zero blinder. The private lane still refuses a sponsored request at its entry, and admitting one needs TWO more parameters on the entry point rather than a relaxation -- the reviewed target, because the sponsor change's program is built from the deployment symbol and refused if a capability offers any other destination, and the sponsor capability itself. The fixture registry has one explicit asset per case and no sponsor-change output role. And the structural one, unchanged and still not a widening of anything: the private lane has NO sponsor signing stage. THE CENSUS GAP IS FILED RATHER THAN INVENTED: the shape census carries no sponsored-shape row vocabulary at all, not one member of it mentioning a sponsor region and every sponsor string in it being sponsorless, so there is nothing there to extend and a member would be new vocabulary arguing past the enum's own stated closure rule -- filed on the census's own discipline that naming a path says nothing about taking it. THE recognize_sponsors MIRROR CLAUSE WAS NOT RE-ADDED, the previous wave's INTENDED verdict being taken as settled. VERIFIED AT THE TIP: conformance 228.8 seconds, transaction 9.4 seconds at 266 of 266, vectors 151.1 seconds at 338 plus 22, tapscript 5.4 seconds at 395 plus 13, every lane also running workspace clippy at deny-warnings and a formatter check; both validators clean with the attestation register byte-stable and zero label diagnostics; and the UNMODIFIED serialized native lane green at 293.1 seconds, 31 of 31, unchanged from the base, so nothing that ran against a real node before this wave stopped doing so. Banned token count ZERO in the diff and ZERO at the tip. |
| `T5-050` | DONE | Sponsor-accept wave: the executor's blinded-sponsor funding stage and the observed acceptance the section 15.2 private-sponsor-values row moves on. The wave opens by taking the previous wave's typed stop as its work list rather than as prose: `A_BLINDED_SPONSOR_VALUE_IS_FUNDED_ANYWHERE` is FALSE and the sites it inventories are what this wave builds. The executor funds a sponsor coin whose VALUE is a commitment and whose ASSET stays EXPLICIT, because the covenant introspects the asset and an introspection reads an explicit field, and the fee stays explicit because consensus defines a fee by its explicitness. The funding transaction needs a BALANCING OUTPUT of its own, its single input being an explicit change coin at a zero blinder and one blinded output being unable to close a sum the input contributes nothing to. The caching gap the template does not close is the one that matters here: `fund_confidential` retains nothing because a receipt is never spent, and a sponsor coin IS spent, so its 33-byte commitment has to survive the funding step and reach the signature hash the signing step takes. The row moves ONLY on an observed acceptance of the sponsored confidential with-change shape at the pinned node, and `may_enter_the_delta` opens for it in the same commit as the observation and never before. Any layer that cannot pass takes a TYPED STOP with its path filed. THE COIN IS FUNDED AND THE FLAG IS TRUE. The executor grew a stage that mines a sponsor coin whose VALUE is a commitment and whose ASSET stays explicit, against a registered case, and a real node holds one. Its funding transaction is accepted and mined, and the commitment the chain reports is the one this workspace derives from published constants and no chain at all -- a first-party recomputation rather than a comparison of the run with itself, and the run stops rather than continuing where the two disagree. `A_BLINDED_SPONSOR_VALUE_IS_FUNDED_ANYWHERE` is therefore TRUE, and the site that funds it is named where the flag is instead of inventoried as absent. The funding transaction needs a BALANCING OUTPUT of its own and the reason is stated where a change to either number has to notice: its single input is the executor's explicit change coin at a zero blinder, so one committed output would have to close that sum with a zero blinder of its own, and a commitment under a zero blinder is a point anybody recomputes from a guessed amount -- the form of a blinded output with none of the hiding. The two reserve coins are funded to the SAME semantic amount under different blinders, so the pair witnesses what a commitment is for, and the run reads two different points off the chain rather than arguing that it would. THE CACHING GAP IS CLOSED BY RETAINING THE FIELD AND NOT THE NUMBER: a signature hash commits to the spent output's value field verbatim, that field has two forms, and a cache holding an integer can only rebuild the explicit one, so both sponsor funding stages now write a field and the signing stage writes it through unchanged. THE CANDIDATE THAT SPENDS THE COIN IS REFUSED, AND THAT IS THE FINDING. The candidate was built, owner-signed, sponsor-signed and offered, and the target answered at consensus before script with its balance check. The workspace's reading of that verdict, stated as a reading rather than as the target's: the reserve sub-equation is the sponsor input against the fee and the change, the input carries a blinder now, both outputs that spend it are explicit and carry none, and nothing in the transaction absorbs the difference -- so the sum cannot close whatever the amounts are. A fee is mandatorily explicit, so the only term that COULD absorb the blinder is the sponsor's CHANGE, and a candidate spending a committed sponsor coin must therefore return COMMITTED change. That is the materializer's shape and not the explicit lane's. THE REFUSAL IS ATTRIBUTABLE AND THE ATTRIBUTION IS MEASURED: a candidate names the coin it spends by outpoint alone, so the sponsor coin's value form is not in the submitted bytes at all, and this submission and the explicit control's are the same shape at the same width -- 1636 bytes and weight 2872, equal on both sides -- differing only in which coin they reach for. A READING RECORDED ELSEWHERE IS OVERTURNED BY RUNNING. `recognize_sponsors` records that the two value forms are independently choosable and that an explicit transfer's sponsor could carry a commitment, filed as merely unbuilt and named as what the `private-sponsor-values` row is about. The first half stands: nothing guards the form, and construction produced the transaction without complaint. The second does not -- the shape is one a target refuses, and the refusal is arithmetic rather than policy. The refusal is asserted rather than tolerated, because an acceptance would mean the arithmetic is wrong. THE SECTION 15.2 ROW DOES NOT MOVE AND `may_enter_the_delta` IS NOT OPENED. The row moves only on an observed acceptance of its own shape, no such shape was offered to a node, and what this wave established is that the explicit route to one is closed by consensus rather than by effort. THE STOP IS AT THE PRIVATE LANE AND IT IS NARROWER THAN THE ONE IT REPLACES, because three of the four sites the previous stop inventoried are now built: the executor funds and signs a committed sponsor coin, the fixture registry holds the sponsor's own reserve case, and the ceremony has a value-form axis that varies ONE thing against the control beside it. What remains is the private lane admitting a sponsored request -- its entry refuses any form but sponsorless and passes a hardcoded sponsor count of zero to shape selection, its openings vocabulary indexes inputs against the receipts alone with no region to describe a sponsor's coin, its destination intents write the protocol asset over every position, and its control assembly builds one witness per receipt with no sponsor slot. The registry narrowing the previous stop named is SMALLER than it looked: a registry holds many cases and each case carries its own asset, so a sponsored successor can reference one case for its protocol-asset destinations and another for its reserve-asset ones, and the per-output asset widening is needed only where one case's outputs would carry two. THE CENSUS GAP STAYS FILED AND UNINVENTED, on the previous wave's own ground. BYTE IDENTITY HOLDS: the explicit with-change control still produces the same identity at this tip, so the demonstration deployment did not move under a wave that added a second value form beside it. |
| `T5-051` | DONE | Private-sponsor wave: sponsorship admitted into the PRIVATE construction lane, and the observed acceptance the section 15.2 `private-sponsor-values` row moves on. The wave opens on the previous wave's typed stop read as a work list rather than as prose, and that stop is narrow because the arithmetic is already answered: a committed sponsor value REQUIRES committed change, the explicit lane cannot carry one, and so the accepting shape can only be the private lane's. Five named sites refuse it today. The private entry point refuses any form but sponsorless and passes a hardcoded sponsor count of zero to shape selection; the openings vocabulary indexes inputs against the receipts alone, with no way to describe a sponsor's coin; the destination intents write the protocol asset over every position, so a reserve-asset change output cannot be stated; and the private control assembly builds one witness per receipt with no sponsor slot. Opening them is the whole of the construction work, and every refusal that exists keeps firing for its own reason on its own mutant -- a guard narrowed is not a guard deleted. The shape then driven to a node is the sponsored CONFIDENTIAL WITH-CHANGE one: a freshly funded blinded sponsor coin whose value is a commitment and whose asset stays explicit, blinded receipt destinations, COMMITTED sponsor change back, and an explicit fee. The row moves ONLY on an observed acceptance of that shape at the pinned node, and `may_enter_the_delta` opens for it in the same commit as the observation and never before. A reading recorded at `recognize_sponsors` is target-refuted and is corrected as part of this wave: it names an explicit transfer's sponsor carrying a commitment as what this row is about, and the previous wave's submission established that such a candidate is refused at consensus. Any layer that cannot pass takes a TYPED STOP with its path filed. THE PRIVATE LANE NOW ADMITS A SPONSORED REQUEST, and the five sites are open. The entry point no longer refuses the form: it calls the EXPLICIT lane's own `check_form_exactness`, so §12.5's equivalence is checked once and both lanes fail it identically, and `PrivateFinalizationIsSponsorless` is dropped rather than narrowed because nothing could raise it any more -- the repository's own precedent for a refusal that has run out of ground. What replaces it is a NARROWER guard the previous vocabulary could not state: openings are indexed across the WHOLE input order, receipts first and the sponsor suffix after, and each opening DECLARES its region and is refused where the declaration disagrees with the position. Declared rather than inferred, on the materializer's own stated ground that a region read off the asset would make the protocol balance depend on a comparison the balance is trying to decide; and checked rather than re-sorted, because openings carry blinders and an opening moved to another position is an opening applied to a coin it does not open. SHAPE SELECTION LEARNED A SECOND DISCOUNT AND THE TWO ARE COUNTED APART. A declared sponsor-change position occupies a destination slot that is not a live receipt output, exactly as a fee position does, so both are subtracted before the receipt-output comparison -- but only the FEE decides whether the candidate bears a fee. Collapsing them into one total is arithmetically equal on every shape this lane builds today and wrong on the first sponsorless request that declares a change position, which is the kind of agreement that stops holding without anything looking different. PER-DESTINATION ASSETS ARE HONORED and the fee is the case that proves they had to be: a SPONSORED candidate's fee and its sponsor change both carry the RESERVE asset, because §10.7 isolates those roles in it and the sponsor's coin brought it in, while a SPONSORLESS candidate's fee is funded from the receipts and carries the PROTOCOL asset. That is one role whose asset the FORM decides, so it could not be a constant beside the role. The sponsor change's program is the deployment's own sponsor-change symbol rather than any owner's constructor, which is the second exception beside the fee and has the same shape of reason: the output repays the sponsor and not a live receipt holder. THE OWNER CENSUS NEEDED NOTHING, and that is a property rather than a gap: `from_proof_finalized` derives its spent outputs from the materializer's signer inputs, which are every input in input order, so a sponsored private candidate's owner message commits to the sponsor's spent coin without a chaining clause of the kind the explicit route had to grow. THE REFUTED READING IS CORRECTED where it stood, at `recognize_sponsors`, citing the observed refusal and naming this lane as the one that builds what the row is about. Package lane green at 266 tests, vectors lane green, and the native serial lane 32/32 at the lane tip -- the same identity re-derives, so admitting sponsorship moved no identity. THE PRIVATE SPONSOR'S SIGNING REQUEST IS MINTED WHERE THE CANDIDATE IS. The first attempt built it in the ceremony and did not compile, because `SponsorSigningRequest::new` is crate-private -- a boundary rather than an oversight: a request names the exact bytes an authorization is produced against, so a caller able to mint one could ask a sponsor to authorize bytes that are not the candidate's. The boundary is kept and the transaction crate grew `private_sponsor_witnesses`, which the private lane needs on its own account and not merely for tidiness: the explicit lane hands a sponsor the WITNESSLESS serialization, and a proof-finalized candidate's protected bytes are the frozen ones the materializer produced, so a sponsor answering the explicit lane's request would be authorizing a transaction that is not this one. It returns witnesses BY POSITION rather than a transaction, because this lane's owner authorizations are produced outside the crate, and merging by position is what keeps either half from displacing the other. THE WITNESS VECTOR IS NOW INDEXED AND NOT APPENDED, which was a latent defect the sponsorless shapes could not expose: witnesses were pushed once per RECEIPT onto a vector sized by the receipt count and handed to the transaction type positionally, so a sponsored candidate would have given a three-input transaction two witnesses. TWO OBSTACLES WERE FOUND BY READING AND ONE DISSOLVED. The declared sponsor-region outputs take their amounts from the REQUEST while the sponsor states the same numbers in its OFFER, and two sources for one number is refused here rather than left to a node, whose balance check would answer truly about arithmetic while saying nothing about a request that asked for two different numbers. The second looked larger: the semantic balance filters created outputs on `is_a_protocol_member`, which is TRUE for the fee role, so a sponsored fee carrying the reserve asset would have been added into a protocol subtotal -- two assets added together. It dissolved on the materializer's own vocabulary. A sponsored fee is EXPLICIT, and the intent already carries a `NonProtocolFundingRegion` whose members are emitted as outputs, excluded from both equations, and whose own documentation names a policy fee and a policy change as its intended members. So the sponsored private candidate declares its receipts and its sponsor change as destinations and puts its FEE in the region, and no new role, no fixture-projection constructor and no materializer surgery is needed. The fee role keeps the meaning it has -- the SPONSORLESS fee funded from the receipts, in the protocol asset -- and stays a protocol member for it. THE WAVE TYPED-STOPS ONE LAYER BELOW THE LANE IT OPENED, AND THE ROW DOES NOT MOVE. No sponsored confidential shape was offered to a node, so `may_enter_the_delta` stays closed and the section 15.2 `private-sponsor-values` row stays unmoved -- the exit-gate rule is that the row moves on an observed acceptance of its OWN shape and on nothing else, and this wave produced none. THE STOP IS AT THE FIXTURE REGISTRY AND IT IS NAMED. `fixture_of` requires EVERY destination to name the same fixture handle, refusing `FixtureOutputOrderMismatch` otherwise, and the binding census beside it requires that one case's output count to equal the destination count and its per-position role, program, amount and asset to agree. A sponsored private successor has three destinations -- two protocol-asset receipt outputs and one RESERVE-asset sponsor change -- so all three must come from ONE registered case declaring two assets across its positions, and the registry cannot state one: a registry output carries a role, a semantic amount and an output program and no asset, the manifest carries a single explicit asset for the whole case, and `FixtureOutputRole` has no sponsor-change member. THE VOCABULARY EXISTS ON THE READING SIDE AND NOTHING WRITES IT, which is the precise shape of the gap rather than a shortage of effort: `ConfidentialFixtureOutputView::sponsor_change` takes a reserve asset and is exactly the projection needed, and the asset comparison already reads `asset_carried` so a sponsor change is not refused for carrying the asset its own fixture declares -- both built where a candidate is READ, and that constructor's only callers in this workspace are two materializer unit tests assembling a view by hand. A hand-assembled view carries no REGISTERED DIGEST, which is the one thing the executor's funding step and the candidate must both bind to. THE PREVIOUS WAVE'S REGISTRY FILING IS OVERTURNED BY READING THE MATERIALIZER. T5-050 recorded the narrowing as smaller than it looked, reasoning that a sponsored successor could reference one case for its protocol-asset destinations and another for its reserve-asset ones; two cases across one destination list is the FIRST thing `fixture_of` refuses. The per-output asset widening that filing called needed only where one case's outputs would carry two assets is needed here, because this case's outputs do. THE WIDENING WAS NOT TAKEN, deliberately and with the reason recorded where the stop is: it lands in a DIGEST TRANSCRIPT. A role's transcript code is documented stable once written because those bytes are inside every registered digest, so adding a code is admissible; a per-output asset is the harder half, since every recorded fixture digest here is evidence a run against a pinned node produced, and a transcript change that moved them would be re-recording evidence to keep a test green. That is a change to make deliberately with its digest-stability argument written down and checked, not a step inside a wave whose subject is the construction lane. The stop is filed at `A_SPONSORED_PRIVATE_SUCCESSOR_IS_REGISTRABLE` in packages/vectors/src/live_sponsor_shapes.rs, false, with the site named, and a test holds it beside the closed gate so the two cannot drift apart. |
| `T5-052` | DONE | Sponsor-registry wave: the LAST registry layer of the sponsor arc, and the observed acceptance the section 15.2 `private-sponsor-values` row moves on. The wave opens on the previous wave's typed stop read as a work list rather than as prose, and the stop is a single sentence: a sponsored private successor is not REGISTRABLE. Every layer beneath it is open — the private construction lane admits sponsored forms, the real sponsor count is kept apart from the fee and sponsor-change discounts, openings are declared by region, destinations carry their own assets, and `assemble_control` fills a position-indexed witness slot — and what remains is that the fixture registry cannot say what this successor IS. `fixture_of` requires every destination to name one fixture handle, a registered case carries a single `explicit_asset`, and a sponsored private successor has two protocol-asset receipt outputs beside a reserve-asset sponsor change; `ConfidentialFixtureOutputView::sponsor_change` already exists on the reading side with nothing in the workspace writing it. THE WIDENING IS ROLE-KEYED, and that is a ruling rather than a preference: a `SponsorChange` member joins the fixture output role vocabulary carrying its OWN asset, and every byte it adds to the digest transcript is emitted UNDER THE NEW ROLE CODE, exactly as the `SoleBalancing` and `Fee` roles were added in the two rows above. The transcript already emits a role code per output unconditionally, so a new variant perturbs nothing a previous manifest hashed, while a manifest-level member or a presence flag would shift every registered digest — and every recorded digest here is evidence a run against a pinned node produced, so moving one would be re-recording evidence to keep a test green. The condition is therefore part of the filing and not a caveat on it: every recorded fixture digest must re-derive BIT-FOR-BIT under the byte-identity tests that already exist, a single fresh digest for the new sponsored case being free; an implementation that moves any recorded digest STOPS and reports rather than re-recording. The row then registers the sponsored private successor case and binds the funded sponsor coin and the candidate to that one registered digest, which is the binding a hand-assembled view lacks, and drives the SPONSORED CONFIDENTIAL WITH-CHANGE shape through the now-open private lane to an OBSERVED ACCEPTANCE: blinded sponsor coin in at an explicit asset, blinded receipt destinations, COMMITTED sponsor change, explicit reserve fee in the non-protocol funding region and outside both balance equations. The row moves ONLY on an observed acceptance of that shape at the pinned node, and `may_enter_the_delta` — which hard-refuses `PrivateSponsorValues` at packages/vectors/src/live_closeout.rs, the exit-gate ruling written as code — opens in the same commit as the observation and never before, alongside the flag at `A_SPONSORED_PRIVATE_SUCCESSOR_IS_REGISTRABLE` and its inventory. Any layer that cannot pass takes a TYPED STOP with its path filed. THE WIDENING LANDED ROLE-KEYED AND NO RECORDED DIGEST MOVED. A `SponsorChange` member carrying its own asset joins the fixture output role vocabulary at transcript code 5, and the asset it names is emitted through the per-output field the transcript ALREADY carried: that field was sourced from the manifest's single explicit asset and is now sourced by role, so no field, flag, position or framing changed and a manifest with no sponsor change hashes the bytes it always hashed. One accessor feeds both the transcript and the value commitment, because a digest framing one asset while the commitment was built against another would describe a fixture nobody could reproduce. The stability is RECOMPUTED rather than asserted: the three byte-identity tests re-derive every recorded digest and pass by name -- `the_run_of_record_fixtures_register_under_the_digests_it_recorded`, `the_shapes_that_ran_before_this_wave_register_under_their_recorded_digests`, and `the_shapes_this_wave_added_carry_digests_of_their_own` -- inside 342 vectors unit tests in 75.2 seconds, beside 20 conformance tests in 196.4 seconds. The serialized native lane then ran the whole live set against a real node at the widened tip and returned 32 of 32 in 293.2 seconds, which is the stronger statement: every confidential shape this workspace has ever accepted reproduced its identity through the changed code. A SILENT SUBSTITUTION WAS CLOSED ON THE WAY: the private lane's role map swept every unnamed role into `Balancing`, and a sponsor change arriving there would have asked the materializer to SOLVE its blinder. A solved blinder absorbs the input sum, so the remainder would have stopped being the sponsor's and the receipts would have stopped balancing -- one substitution producing two wrong outputs, and a candidate that is wrong rather than refused. THE FEE'S PLACEMENT IS DECIDED ON THE CODE RATHER THAN ON THE PROSE, and the two disagreed. `check_sponsor_region_amounts` states that the private lane declares its fee and its sponsor change as destination positions, and `private_destination_intents` carries a sponsored arm that gives a fee destination the RESERVE asset. That arm cannot reach an accepted candidate as written, and two clauses two layers below it are why: a fixture's fee output projects with no reserve asset, so `asset_carried` returns the case's PROTOCOL asset and the preflight asset comparison refuses a reserve-asset fee destination with `ProtocolAssetMismatch`; and `Fee` answers `is_a_protocol_member` with true, so the same output would be summed into the protocol created subtotal and add reserve units to a protocol tally. The sponsored fee therefore rides in `NonProtocolFundingRegion`, whose members are emitted as explicit outputs, held outside both balance equations, and checked against carrying the protocol asset and against colliding with a destination's program -- every clause of which this shape satisfies. The successor case declares THREE outputs and not four. The unreachable sponsored fee arm is recorded as a finding rather than repaired here, its repair being a vocabulary question this row does not own. THE SHAPE IS ACCEPTED AND THE ROW IS MOVED. A real node accepted and mined the sponsored CONFIDENTIAL with-change successor, 13873 bytes at weight 15490 in 19.2 seconds: a blinded sponsor coin in at an explicit asset, two blinded receipt destinations, a COMMITTED sponsor change in the reserve asset, and an explicit reserve fee in the non-protocol funding region and outside both balance equations. The bytes the node reported back are the bytes it was handed, an owner's signature verified against a message recomputed independently of the candidate, and the sponsor's committed change was located in the MINED bytes rather than assumed from what was sent. The blinded sponsor coin is censused by NAMED CHECK and never by a scalar, all four holding, the load-bearing one being that the commitment the chain holds is the one this workspace derives from published constants and no chain at all. TWO WALLS STOOD BETWEEN THE REGISTRY AND THE CHAIN AND NEITHER WAS THE REGISTRY'S, which is the disclosure this row owes most. The first: this ceremony seeded the owner message with the genesis identity in the order a target PRINTS a block identity rather than the order it HASHES one, and the node returned its own invalid-signature verdict while every consensus balance check passed -- the exact `DeploymentSeedInPrintedOrder` negative control this workspace had already measured, reproduced by accident and identified by the control that existed for it. That a wrong-preimage message and a balance-valid transaction are distinguishable at a node is what made the diagnosis a reading rather than a guess. The second: a sponsored request bears a fee by construction, so the candidate EXECUTES the covenant's fee clause, and the demonstration deployment is welded to a fixture fee digest that no program hashes to -- so the clause demanded of the fee output a value nothing can present and the node refused at OP_EQUALVERIFY. That is the defect the sponsor arc met on the explicit lane, met here for the same reason and answered the same way, by linking the FEE-BEARING vocabulary, whose taptree is its own; the demonstration's committed identity did not move to buy it. WHAT MOVED, IN ONE COMMIT WITH THE OBSERVATION: the section 15.2 `private-sponsor-values` row, `may_enter_the_delta` -- which hard-refused that class as the exit-gate ruling written in code -- and `A_SPONSORED_PRIVATE_SUCCESSOR_IS_REGISTRABLE`, now true with its inventory rewritten to say what the stop was and what answered it. The test that held the closed pair together now holds the open one, and it holds the gate against the accepted identity so a gate opened with no run behind it fails. THREE FINDINGS ARE FILED RATHER THAN REPAIRED HERE, each outside this row's subject. First, the sponsored branch of `private_destination_intents`' fee arm -- which gives a fee DESTINATION the reserve asset when the form is sponsored -- cannot reach an accepted candidate as written, being refused two layers below by the preflight asset comparison and by the fee role's protocol membership; it has never run, and whether it should be widened or removed is a vocabulary question. Second, NOTHING CROSS-CHECKS THE NON-PROTOCOL REGION MEMBER against the shape's own fee position, asset, or empty program: this candidate's fee landed at the index the covenant introspects because the materializer appends region members after the destinations and the arithmetic happened to agree, not because any clause required it, so a shape whose fee position was not last would build a candidate the covenant refuses with nothing between the two saying why. Third, `link_and_register`'s Demonstration arm still hands out the fixture fee digest, so any FUTURE sponsored ceremony that keeps that vocabulary meets the same OP_EQUALVERIFY wall for the same reason; the arm is correct for sponsorless demonstration work and the hazard is that sponsorship implies a fee without saying so at the link site. The executor's Python digest mirror is a fourth, narrower one: it knows role codes 1 and 2 only and frames one asset for every output, so it could not resolve a case carrying the new role -- which costs nothing today because its catalogue holds only the three one-asset predecessor and sponsor-coin cases and a successor never reaches it, and which would bite the moment a sponsored case had to be funded rather than merely submitted. |
| `T5-053` | DONE | Explicit-fee wave: the LAST cell of the owner fee matrix, the SPONSORLESS EXPLICIT transfer that pays its own fee, driven to an observed acceptance of its own shape. Three cells were observed already -- sponsored-explicit, sponsored-confidential, and the confidential sponsorless one-to-one-plus-fee -- and this is the fourth and last. THE GAP WAS NOT WHERE THE FEE AXIS WAS, IT WAS WHERE THE FEE COULD BE DECLARED. The axis, the fee-bearing vocabulary and ruling R-C's conservation leaf were all already landed; what no request could do was SELECT a fee-bearing shape from the explicit lane, because the explicit entry point passed a hardcoded empty role census where the private lane censuses its openings. So the leaf that carries the fee as a TERM had been emitted at link time by a vocabulary nothing could reach and had NEVER been executed at a node, and this wave is the first request that ran it. THE DESIGN QUESTION THE GUIDE ANSWERED, not the design memo: section 12.3's may-not-select list names the target fee role and section 12.5 says a sponsorless request selects no fee-sponsor role, so a request-level fee amount was refused as a vocabulary change the guide forbids. The private lane had already answered it the other way and this wave copied that answer -- the fee is a DESTINATION ENTRY, which section 12.3 does admit, and its ROLE is declared beside the entry at construction time exactly as an opening's role is. The new `ExplicitDestinationRole` is that declaration with the confidential half removed rather than a reuse of `ConfidentialOutputRole`, whose members are blinding distinctions an explicit destination does not draw. THE CONSERVATION TERM TURNED OUT TO NEED NO CODE, and the reason is structural rather than lucky: because the fee IS a destination entry it is already inside the created total the request states, so `consumed == created` closes unchanged and a fee appended by construction would have been the case needing a term added back. Ruling R-C's leaf is unaffected -- it reads the fee at the fee position in the COVENANT, which is a different equality from the builder's. ZERO CHURN AT FORTY-FOUR CALL SITES: `finalize_live_transfer` became a wrapper delegating to `finalize_live_transfer_declaring` with an empty declaration, so one pipeline serves both and a self-paying candidate is not a parallel construction path that could drift from the one every other explicit shape uses. An empty declaration means every entry is a receipt output, which is what the other thirteen shapes say, and their runs build the bytes they built before. THE FEE IS THE PROTOCOL ASSET, mirroring the confidential lane's own rule at the same site: the fee is funded out of the consumed receipts, Elements balances per asset, and a reserve-asset fee beside no reserve-asset input dies at the tally. The sponsored fee keeps the reserve for the mirror-image reason. THE FOURTEENTH SHAPE IS NOT A SECTION 15 ROW and says so in its own type. Section 15.1's explicit positive table is complete at sixteen and a self-paying transfer is none of the sixteen classes, so `row_name` became an OPTION and this shape answers None -- the same shape the confidential ceremony's row_name has, for the same reason. A test asserts EXACTLY ONE shape names no row, so a second one acquiring the absent case is loud rather than invisible. The collision census keeps counting ten identities across the thirteen row-naming shapes and additionally checks that the fourteenth's identity collides with none of them, which is the property the census exists to protect: one acceptance may never be cited for work it did not do. THE NODE TOOK IT ON THE FIRST RUN and the run is REPRODUCIBLE -- the standalone drive and the full serialized lane produced the same identity bit-for-bit. One receipt of 5000 consumed, one destination of 4750 created and a fee of 250 paid with no sponsor anywhere in it, 782 bytes submitted and the mined bytes read back EQUAL to them, the single input's signature verified against an independently recomputed message, target-reported weight 1304, 6.0 seconds of wall time. THE TRANSACTION-VERSION FILING IS ANSWERED BY RUNNING AND DISCHARGED BY KEEPING THE VERSION. This is the first sponsorless form to face relay on its own; its predecessors paid no fee and travelled as package children, which is why the ABI builds a sponsorless form at the topology-restricted version. The candidate was offered to `testmempoolaccept` at that version with NO package parent and the mempool ALLOWED it, a block then including it. So the premise was narrower than it looked and the version is right anyway: what the topology-restricted version restricts is unconfirmed ancestry and descendants, and a transaction with neither satisfies those restrictions trivially rather than by exemption. Travelling as a package child was one way to satisfy them and not the reason the version was chosen. The claim is held by a test that recomputes the version off the built transaction rather than by the comment that discharges it. ONE MEASUREMENT GAP CLOSED IN PASSING: the explicit ceremony recorded no weight at all though the adapter had been reporting one, so section 20.5's comparison had nothing to compare on this lane. The record now carries the TARGET's figure, read off `decoderawtransaction` rather than computed here. NO CENSUS ROW MOVED AND NONE WAS INVENTED: the shape census is over BLINDED shapes and has no slot for an explicit-lane fee shape, so the evidence surface is the run of record and this row, as the brief directed. Every recorded digest re-derives untouched and that is a RUN rather than a claim -- the fee-bearing vocabulary is a SEPARATE deployment, so the demonstration taptree and every identity recorded against it are unmoved, and a test asserts only this shape leaves the demonstration vocabulary. Verified entirely on the server per the no-host-compute ruling, every step timed. |
| `T5-054` | DONE | Crossing wave: both REPRESENTATION-CROSSING directions of the ruling, each driven to an observed acceptance of its own shape. The fee matrix is complete and this wave leaves it alone; what is open is that a live transfer's representation is ONE variable for the whole transaction, so an explicit-valued receipt may not be spent into a blinded destination nor a blinded receipt into an explicit one, though the target admits both and this workspace's own predecessor funding performs the first one every ceremony as a FUNDING step rather than as a covenant-governed transfer. DIRECTION ONE is entry-blinding: explicit receipt inputs into TWO OR MORE blinded receipt outputs, two being the floor because a single blinded output is the zero-blinder degeneracy the fixture registry refuses as `DegenerateBalancingScalar` and Elements' own wallet refuses for the same reason -- a convention about confidentiality that both sides hold rather than a consensus rule, and the register's escape analysis stays as it is. DIRECTION TWO is exit-unblinding: blinded receipt inputs into explicit destination outputs beside the BLINDED ABSORBER that a nonzero input blinder sum requires, the absorber's blinder solved to that sum by the machinery the merge wave already proved. The absorber is a DECLARED DESTINATION POSITION and not a fourth output family, which is the ruling this wave is executed under: an absorber outside the destination range would carry the protocol asset outside that range and falsify the closure argument the whole induction rests on, while a declared position leaves the closure untouched and is checked by the leaf that declared it. Guide section 6.5 says mixed representation is unsupported UNLESS SEPARATELY ADMITTED, so the ruling IS that admission and no guide amendment is sought; the compiler's `MixedComposition` deferral is NARROWED to per-reference composition within one side rather than deleted, its ground surviving the wave literally true because the crossing introduces a per-side variable and still no per-reference one, and both negative safety rows keep refusing for their own stated reasons. The CONFIDENTIAL-TO-FEE-ONLY corner stays SOURCE-DERIVED-IMPOSSIBLE and is not attempted. Each direction moves only on a real acceptance at the pinned node -- funded, submitted, offered to `testmempoolaccept` before the mine, mined bytes read back EQUAL, signatures verified against independently recomputed per-input messages -- and the absorber's facts are censused by NAMED BOOLEAN CHECK and never by a scalar. Any layer that cannot pass takes a TYPED STOP with its path filed rather than being narrated around. THE EXIT DIRECTION IS ACCEPTED: two blinded receipts consumed, two EXPLICIT destinations created and one blinded absorber beside them, 5412 bytes submitted at a target-reported weight of 6561, the mined bytes read back EQUAL to them and an owner signature verified against a message recomputed independently of the candidate, in 11.5 seconds. It is the first transaction this workspace has built whose consumed and created sides are read under DIFFERENT representation plans. THE CROSSING IS VISIBLE IN THE BYTES and not only in the construction: the output-witness proof census is zero, zero and 4174 bytes, because an explicit value admits no range proof and an explicit asset no surjection proof, so the two explicit destinations carry neither and the single blinded absorber carries the transaction's only proof -- a vector no homogeneous shape of that arity can produce, three proofs being the wholly private answer and none the wholly explicit one. THE ADMISSION IS THE GUIDE'S OWN CLAUSE RATHER THAN AN AMENDMENT: section 6.5 says mixed is unsupported UNLESS SEPARATELY ADMITTED, and its next sentence is why no run could ever have supplied the admission -- an ad hoc mixed transaction accepted by the target does not widen the ABI -- so only a ruling could, and the owner's is it. What the ruling admits is a PAIRING of the two plans section 6.1 states exhaustively, one per SIDE, so the plan census stays at two members and crossing introduces no third representation. `MixedComposition` is NARROWED and not deleted: what stays deferred is two references of ONE side disagreeing, and its ground `NoPerReferenceVariable` stays literally true and still re-derives from the analyzed program, because a side is not a reference. Both negative safety rows keep refusing for their own stated reasons. THE CONSTRUCTOR DERIVES ITS REPRESENTATION FROM THE CONSUMED SIDE rather than storing one beside the composition, and that is what keeps the destination table's key where it is: a coin is looked up by the plan that RECOGNIZES it, so a crossing deployment substitutes its crossing constructor at exactly that key and no table widens. A crossing deployment is its own deployment with its own taptree, exactly as the fee-bearing one is, so no identity recorded against the demonstration could move to buy it -- and a coin a crossing transfer creates stays spendable by an ordinary homogeneous one afterwards, which is what makes the crossing a step in a lifecycle rather than a cul-de-sac. THE ABSORBER IS A DECLARED DESTINATION POSITION, checked by a POSITIONAL value-form leaf requiring the explicit form at every destination but the declared one and the confidential form at that one. It is inside the destination range and adds no output family, so the section 10.4 closure argument is untouched -- and the memo's reason for that was REFUTED while its conclusion held: a protocol-asset output outside the destination range already exists, the self-paying fee, and the closure survives it; the real bar is that the exact output count and the three-family position census leave no room for a fourth kind of position at all. Its blinder is not chosen and not stated: with no output deriving one there is nothing to subtract, so the registry's solve returns the consumed blinder sum ITSELF, which is why the pair must not cancel and why this shape spends the merge's own non-canceling predecessor. THE ABSORBER'S FACTS ARE CENSUSED IN BOOLEANS AND NEVER IN A SCALAR: the consumed pair does not cancel, the proof census has exactly one proving entry, the mined bytes equal the submitted ones, and the signature verifies against an independently recomputed message. TWO DEFECTS WERE FOUND BY RUNNING AND BOTH WERE THE DESIGN WORKING LOUDLY. The relocation probe rebuilt its subject through the HOMOGENEOUS entry point, so a crossing constructor's probe emitted a different value obligation, the instruction counts diverged, and the probe reported the owner public key as unlocatable when what actually differed was the covenant; a probe exists to differ from its subject in ONE symbol, and it refused rather than silently recording a relocation at a wrong offset. The pattern census declared the private form while the coordinator emitted the crossing one, leaving the crossing bytes unaccounted; the exit crossing's obligation now rides its OWN pattern identity, because it establishes MORE than the private form does -- which position carries which form rather than one answer for the whole range -- and the three obligations are a PARTITION over compositions rather than over plans. THE REGISTRY ROLE IS THE FEE'S OPPOSITE CORNER, at transcript code 6 and nothing else: a fee is defined by having NO program and an explicit destination by having one, and every role that existed answered at least one predicate wrongly for it. Its arithmetic was RUN before any node: the absorber's solved blinder is the consumed sum itself, the explicit destinations carry no opening, a canceling predecessor is refused as the same degeneracy the register already holds, and the fully-unblinding corner is refused independently by the registry with the unique-balancing-role refusal reporting a found count of zero. THE CENSUS DOMAIN WAS WIDENED AND THE OLD CLOSURE RULE WAS NARROWER THAN IT READ: "at least one blinded input" was not brevity but the premise that let the blinded-output count be DERIVED as outputs-less-fee, and crossing breaks both halves in opposite directions -- an entry crossing consumes no blinded input and an exit crossing carries explicit NON-fee outputs the subtraction would have counted as blinded, computing absorbable for a shape by counting outputs that absorb nothing. The count is now a stated axis, the explicit destinations are the leftover, a test requires the three to partition the output set, and the tally predicate learned the second way a sum lands: a shape with no blinded input presents a sum already zero. THE FULLY-UNBLINDING CORNER IS FILED AND NOT ATTEMPTED, and it is the case the register exists to keep apart -- both sides refuse it and for DIFFERENT reasons, consensus because a nonzero sum has nowhere to land and the registry independently because a manifest with no solving role has no output to solve. THE ENTRY DIRECTION IS ACCEPTED TOO: ONE EXPLICIT receipt consumed and TWO blinded destinations created, 9133 bytes submitted at a target-reported weight of 10093, the mined bytes read back EQUAL and the signature verified against an independently recomputed message, in 9.9 seconds. This workspace has performed the SHAPE every ceremony, as the funding step that mints a confidential predecessor; what the identity records is the first time the coin it spent sat at a RECEIPT CONSTRUCTOR'S PROGRAM, so the transfer was governed by the covenant rather than by the adapter -- which is the whole difference between a funding step and a transfer, and why an accepted funding transaction could never have stood in for this row. Its proof census is 4174 and 4174 bytes and the COUNT is the claim: a single blinded output would have been forced to a ZERO blinder, an explicit input contributing one, and its commitment would have hidden nothing. WHAT THE ENTRY DIRECTION NEEDED WAS NOT CONSENSUS but three first-party readings that had each conflated a SIDE with the transaction, and all three were found by RUNNING rather than by reading. A consumed receipt could not be stated without naming a registered confidential fixture output, though an explicit coin has no opening to name; it now carries an ABSENT opening and is checked against what it IS -- the value really explicit, the amount the one declared, the asset explicit -- with a COMMITTED value and no opening refused rather than read as explicit. The randomness rule keyed on the REQUEST'S plan rather than on the side being blinded, and so refused randomness to the one transfer that most needs it; randomness is what blinding consumes and blinding happens on the side a transfer CREATES, and while both sides were one plan the rule could read either and be right. The opening-binding census counted verified references against EVERY input rather than against the inputs that have them, demanding a reference for a coin with nothing to reference. THE ABSORBER FACTS AND THE ENTRY FACTS ARE BOTH CENSUSED IN BOOLEANS: the exit pair does not cancel, the entry coin carried no blinder, each proof census has exactly the proving entries its shape allows, both read-backs equal their submissions and both signatures verify. EVERY RECORDED DIGEST RE-DERIVES BIT-FOR-BIT and that is a RUN rather than a claim -- all byte-identity tests pass by name at the tip, and the crossing deployments are their own, so the demonstration taptree and every identity recorded against it are unmoved. BOTH RUNS ARE REPRODUCIBLE: each crossing produced the same identity bit-for-bit in a standalone drive and in the full serialized lane, and a measurement gap was closed on the way -- the confidential ceremony recorded no weight at all though the adapter had been reporting one, so section 20.5's comparison had nothing to compare on this lane. THE CROSSING REMOVAL IS THE FIRST THAT FREED TWO SHAPES AND SAW BOTH RUN, recorded as ONE removal with one proof and TWO rows citing it rather than as two removals, because what was removed was one reading. |

| `T5-056` | DONE | Timelock-closure wave: the LAST carried residual and the three evidence gaps the minimality wave named, each driven to a verdict its own defining site's rule supports. The wave opens on four named items and claims nothing beyond them. `PredecessorConstructorAbsent` is the one residual the campaign still carries, and it is carried because no time-locked predecessor constructor exists — the time-locked-input safety row has nothing to spend, so the row is blocked on a constructor this workspace has not built rather than on a target verdict. Clearing it needs a constructor and an executor funding stage for a time-locked coin, and then an ATTRIBUTABLE observed result: the locked spend REFUSED naming the lock, and the same shape after maturity ACCEPTED, the accepted control being what makes the refusal attributable to the lock rather than to any other property of the candidate. Projection-equality-with-paired-explicit wants a run comparing the public protocol projections of an accepted private transaction and its paired accepted explicit one, and the rule at its defining site decides whether the existing one-to-one pair's two acceptances satisfy it or whether more is owed. Any layer that cannot pass takes a TYPED STOP with its path filed. THE SPLIT PAIR IS CLOSED. The private lane grew a pure-split shape -- one receipt consumed, TWO created, both of them receipts, no change role and no fee -- and a real node ACCEPTED it, 9136 bytes read back equal and the target reporting weight 10096 in 14.1 seconds. What makes the acceptance this member's rather than another near miss is measured rather than argued: the run carried TWO output-witness range proofs over TWO created outputs, and a fee output carries no range proof at all, so two proofs over two outputs is the measured form of the conjunct's own words. The shape is APPENDED to the shape array rather than seated beside the split it is a sibling of, because the byte-identity tests pin each recorded successor digest at its index and an insertion would have renumbered every digest after it -- making a test that exists to catch drift report drift that did not happen. No §15.2 row moved: `private-split` moved on the three-output split and a row does not move twice. The registry recomputes to FOUR of five pairs supporting, and the report keeps the weaker standing token because one pair is still short. PROJECTION-EQUALITY IS A TYPED STOP, and the stop is filed at the row's own site as `A_PAIRED_ACCEPTED_PROJECTION_COMPARISON_EXISTS`, FALSE. The wave was pointed at the one-to-one pair's two acceptances on the reading that both members are accepted and only the comparison is missing. Reading the rule refuted it, and TWO things stand in the way rather than one. The first is that those two acceptances are NOT a pair: §16.1 requires a pair to begin from ONE semantic fixture materialized twice, and the explicit one-to-one and the private strict one-to-one are two INDEPENDENT ceremonies whose shapes merely match. The registry already says so in its own bytes -- `PairTargetVerdict` carries no accepted variant at all, and its shape-accepted-elsewhere member exists precisely to record that a run of a member's OWN SHAPE was accepted while denying that the member was -- so comparing them would be the substitution the row's own rule forbids. The second is that no standing can hold the result: `NativeRunObserved` carries exactly ONE accepted identity and a projection equality is a relation over TWO, which is the same narrowness `DeterminismObserved` was minted for, and that minting was DIRECTED in answer to a reported gap rather than taken unilaterally. Closing it needs one run submitting the pair's two MEMBERS -- not their shape siblings -- to one node with both accepted, and a standing able to carry two identities; the pairs lane submits nothing today, so the first half is a capability yet to be built and not a target verdict anybody has seen. THE SPONSOR PAIR IS CLOSED AND THE REGISTRY IS FIVE OF FIVE. The private lane grew an explicit-sponsor shape -- a sponsor coin funded to EXACTLY the fee, one blinded destination declaring the fully-solved form, no change role anywhere -- and a real node ACCEPTED it, 5122 bytes read back equal at weight 5935 in 14.2 seconds, a THIRD of the committed run's bytes because one blinded output carries one range proof where three carry three. The arc's ruling that a COMMITTED sponsor value requires committed change was never violated and never reached this shape: an explicit coin brings the all-zero blinder, so no term is owed one to absorb. The change is ruled out by ARITHMETIC over the acceptance rather than by inspection -- the reserve sub-equation is sponsor-input equals fee plus change, this coin was funded to exactly the fee, and a node that accepted it cannot have been handed a change output -- which is stronger than the byte scan the record used to run, the fee carrying the reserve asset too; that check is now ASKED ONLY of a shape that declares a change role. Reaching the acceptance peeled ONE first-party defect, latent and unreachable until now: the opening-free input path built an `explicit_receipt` whatever region the caller declared, so a sponsor coin with an explicit value became a RECEIPT silently and its amount joined the protocol subtotal §1.9 holds it outside of, refusing a candidate whose arithmetic was right with an imbalance of exactly the sponsor's own amount. No earlier shape could expose it: every opening-free input before this was an entry crossing's real receipt, and every sponsor coin before this carried a commitment. The bare path now takes the SAME declared region the opened path does. The minimality report's standing moves to the stronger token, and it inherits rather than escapes the disclosure that no pair MEMBER was submitted -- what each cites is an acceptance of its own SHAPE. DIGEST AUDIT: the pre-existing sponsored private run reproduced at this tip, its own recorded identity, and the demonstration taptree never moved. THE RESIDUAL IS NOT CLEARED, AND THE ROW IS MIS-TYPED. `PredecessorConstructorAbsent` stays carried, and the wave reports WHY rather than building around it: the `time-locked-input` row cannot be answered at the boundary it declares, so an observation filed against it would be filed under a layer that did not happen. Two gaps, and the second decides it. The first is the one the residual already names -- no time-locked constructor exists, `ObjectId::ReceiptTimeLocked` being a fully specified covenant branch that `derive_class` nonetheless refuses because it pins the protocol object to `ReceiptLive`. The second is that building one would not produce the row's evidence anyway: the live covenant has NO input-class check to violate. The live class is carried by CONSTRUCTOR TYPING and emits ZERO instructions, no live fragment emits an input scriptPubKey introspection at all, and the two receipt classes carry the SAME asset -- so a time-locked receipt passes every comparison the leaf makes. What enforces the distinction is the LEAF COMMITMENT, so the spend fails `VerifyTaprootCommitment` BEFORE any opcode runs, and the target says mandatory-script-verify-flag-failed with a witness program hash mismatch. The row declares the SCRIPT-PATH boundary, whose vocabulary member means the target RAN the script path and it failed; nothing ran, and filing the adapter's text-prefix classification under it would repeat the mis-filing already recorded against the key-path probe. The refusal would not be ATTRIBUTABLE either -- it attributes to a leaf not committed by the spent program, which is true of any foreign taptree, so a live-predecessor control beside it would differ in which TREE the coin sits in rather than in which CLASS was recognized. A ruling is owed on which of two repairs the row wants: move its boundary to the layer that really refuses a cross-class predecessor, after which a constructor and a funding stage are the whole of the work; or accept §7.3 as discharged by constructor typing and retype the row first-party, as its `time-locked-output` sibling already is. Filed at the row's own site as `THE_TIME_LOCKED_INPUT_ROW_IS_REACHABLE_AS_TYPED`, FALSE. |

So the deliverable is an EXPLICIT KNOWLEDGE MAP and not a widening: an honest expressible-and-unrun, or a refusal at a named site with a removal path filed, is a COMPLETE answer for a cell, and driving new acceptances is out of scope. The last is a member minted by this wave: the sponsor axis turned out to contain a form somebody had BUILT and offered, which a node refused on the balance rule itself, and the register had nowhere to put a refusal that strong. That derivation independently reproduces the two recorded sponsor facts, the committed-value refusal and the explicit-value acceptance, as two halves of one rule. THE WIDER FEE-BEARING ARITIES ARE EXPRESSIBLE AND UNRUN, proven rather than assumed: the fixture registry places no cardinality rule on the fee role and no ceiling on the output count, and the reviewed shape vocabulary carries a sponsorless fee-bearing member at every count in its bounds, both LINKED and driven in tests; one such arity has run and five have not. Three walls the PRODUCT found are labeled with sites and paths, one of them explicitly not wanted removed, because a register able to record only walls it wished gone would stop recording the other kind. Two claims were REFUTED BY DRIVING and corrected rather than kept: the plain reading that an exit crossing without its absorber is impossible is false beside a committed sponsor change, which keeps the tally balanced while leaving no SOLVING role, so consensus admits a form this workspace cannot state; and clippy's rejection of a nested option was right about the modelling, the registry admitting a manifest and the registry not being the authority on one being different facts.
